=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
Given a string s of length n and an integer k, determine whether it is possible to select k disjoint special substrings.
A special substring is a substring where:

Any character present inside the substring should not appear outside it in the string.
The substring is not the entire string s.

Note that all k substrings must be disjoint, meaning they cannot overlap.
Return true if it is possible to select k such disjoint special substrings; otherwise, return false.
 
Example 1:

Input: s = "abcdbaefab", k = 2
Output: true
Explanation:

We can select two disjoint special substrings: "cd" and "ef".
"cd" contains the characters 'c' and 'd', which do not appear elsewhere in s.
"ef" contains the characters 'e' and 'f', which do not appear elsewhere in s.


Example 2:

Input: s = "cdefdc", k = 3
Output: false
Explanation:
There can be at most 2 disjoint special substrings: "e" and "f". Since k = 3, the output is false.

Example 3:

Input: s = "abeabe", k = 0
Output: true

 
Constraints:

2 <= n == s.length <= 5 * 10^4
0 <= k <= 26
s consists only of lowercase English letters.

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def maxSubstringLength(self, s: str, k: int) -> bool:
        
```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Now, check special condition using positions lists:
def is_special(l,r):
    if l==0 and r==n-1: return False
    for c in range(26):
        arr = pos[c]
        if not arr: continue
        # find first occurrence >= l
        i = bisect_left(arr, l)
        if i < len(arr) and arr[i] <= r:
            # char appears in interval
            if first[c] < l or last[c] > r:
                return False
    return True

Implementation details:
class Solution:
    def maxSubstringLength(self, s: str, k: int) -> bool:
        from bisect import bisect_left
        n = len(s)
        if k == 0:
            return True
        first = [-1]*26
        last = [-1]*26
        pos = [[] for _ in range(26)]
        for i,ch in enumerate(s):
            idx = ord(ch)-97
            if first[idx] == -1:
                first[idx] = i
            last[idx] = i
            pos[idx].append(i)
        starts = [first[c] for c in range(26) if first[c] != -1]
        ends = [last[c] for c in range(26) if last[c] != -1]
        intervals = []
        for l in starts:
            for r in ends:
                if l > r:
                    continue
                if l == 0 and r == n-1:
                    continue
                # check special
                ok = True
                for c in range(26):
                    arr = pos[c]
                    if not arr:
                        continue
                    # if char appears in [l,r]
                    i = bisect_left(arr, l)
                    if i < len(arr) and arr[i] <= r:
                        if first[c] < l or last[c] > r:
                            ok = False
                            break
                if ok:
                    intervals.append((l,r))
        # max disjoint intervals
        intervals.sort(key=lambda x: x[1])
        count = 0
        end = -1
        for l,r in intervals:
            if l > end:
                count += 1
                end = r
        return count >= k

Need think about if k can be > maximum possible but k<=26. Good.

Let's write final code:

```python
from bisect import bisect_left
from typing import List? Not needed.

class Solution:
    def maxSubstringLength(self, s: str, k: int) -> bool:
        n = len(s)
        if k == 0:
            return True

        first = [-1] * 26
        last = [-1] * 26
        positions = [[] for _ in range(26)]

        for i, ch in enumerate(s):
            c = ord(ch) - ord('a')
            if first[c] == -1:
                first[c] = i
            last[c] = i
            positions[c].append(i)

        starts = [first[c] for c in range(26) if first[c] != -1]
        ends = [last[c] for c in range(26) if last[c] != -1]

        intervals = []
        for l in starts:
            for r in ends:
                if l > r:
                    continue
                if l == 0 and r == n - 1:
                    continue

                ok = True
                for c in range(26):
                    arr = positions[c]
                    if not arr:
                        continue
                    # If this character occurs in [l, r], its whole span must be inside.
                    idx = bisect_left(arr, l)
                    if idx < len(arr) and arr[idx] <= r:
                        if first[c] < l or last[c] > r:
                            ok = False
                            break
                if ok:
                    intervals.append((l, r))

        intervals.sort(key=lambda x: x[1])
        cnt = 0
        end = -1
        for l, r in intervals:
            if l > end:
                cnt += 1
                end = r

        return cnt >= k
```
